It’s been scaring me.\n\nDoctor: Do these blank moments seem to happen when you're under a lot of stress or pressure?\n\nPatient: Yes, it does feel worse when there’s pressure. Like if I’m looking at the road-closure forms or I’ve got the stop-slow paddle in my hand, my mind just goes blank, and I suddenly can’t remember steps I’ve done for years. That’s what scares me.\n\nDoctor: Do these blank moments and memory losses last for a while after the pressure or stress is gone?\n\nPatient: It can last a bit, yeah. Even after the pressure eases, I’m still kind of stuck, like my mind hasn’t caught up. I’ll be looking at the forms or the paddle and I just can’t remember the steps I’ve done for years, and it takes me a while to feel clear again.\n\nDoctor: Are you experiencing these memory blanks or difficulties recalling familiar information in situations outside of your job as well?\n\nPatient: Mostly I notice it at work. That’s where it really scares me—like I’ll look at the road-closure forms or be holding the stop-slow paddle, and I suddenly can’t remember steps I’ve done for years. Outside of work, I’m not as sure, but the big blank moments are happening there.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel anxious or overwhelmed in situations outside of your job?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I mean, I do get stressed outside work sometimes, like anyone, but I haven’t noticed the same kind of overwhelming feeling the way I do at work. It’s harder for me to say.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel anxious when you're not at work?\n\nPatient: Not really that I can clearly say. I mean, I get tense sometimes when I’m not working, but I haven’t noticed that same anxious, overwhelmed feeling the way it happens at work.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel anxious when you're not working?\n\nPatient: I don’t really think so. I mean, I can get uneasy here and there, but I haven’t noticed it being a regular thing when I’m not working.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Never bundle several symptoms, or several attributes of one symptom, into a single question — e.g. asking whether a symptom is present and how long it has lasted at the same time.\n- If you need to ask about more than one thing, ask the one that tells you the most now and come back to the rest later.\n- Keep the question clear enough for the patient to understand.\n- Respond in English.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"intent\": \"<what you want to find out with this question>\", \"question\": \"<how you actually ask it in the interview>\"}\n"
  },

    "role": "user",
    "content": "Return the JSON now."

That’s what scares me.\n\nDoctor: Do these blank moments and memory losses last for a while after the pressure or stress is gone?\n\nPatient: It can last a bit, yeah. Even after the pressure eases, I’m still kind of stuck, like my mind hasn’t caught up. I’ll be looking at the forms or the paddle and I just can’t remember the steps I’ve done for years, and it takes me a while to feel clear again.\n\nDoctor: Are you experiencing these memory blanks or difficulties recalling familiar information in situations outside of your job as well?\n\nPatient: Mostly I notice it at work. That’s where it really scares me—like I’ll look at the road-closure forms or be holding the stop-slow paddle, and I suddenly can’t remember steps I’ve done for years. Outside of work, I’m not as sure, but the big blank moments are happening there.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel anxious or overwhelmed in situations outside of your job?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I mean, I do get stressed outside work sometimes, like anyone, but I haven’t noticed the same kind of overwhelming feeling the way I do at work. It’s harder for me to say.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel anxious when you're not at work?\n\nPatient: Not really that I can clearly say. I mean, I get tense sometimes when I’m not working, but I haven’t noticed that same anxious, overwhelmed feeling the way it happens at work.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel anxious when you're not working?\n\nPatient: I don’t really think so. I mean, I can get uneasy here and there, but I haven’t noticed it being a regular thing when I’m not working.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any other problems with your body, like numbness, weakness, or difficulty with coordination?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think so. I haven’t noticed numbness or weakness, and I’m not aware of any coordination problems.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you're outside of your body watching yourself when these blank moments happen?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think so. I wouldn’t describe it like I’m outside my body watching myself. It’s more just… blank, like I lose track for a bit.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel disconnected from yourself or your surroundings when these blank moments are not happening?\n\nPatient: Not that I’ve noticed. I mean, outside of those blank times, I usually feel like I’m just here and aware of what’s around me. I don’t really feel disconnected like that.\n\nDoctor: Have these memory blanks affected your relationships or daily activities outside of your job?\n\nPatient: Yes. It’s been a problem. I wasn’t reliable at work anymore because I couldn’t finish the road-closure paperwork, and I didn’t trust myself with the traffic-safety steps.\n\nOutside of work, I pulled back from things too. I stopped going to choir and volunteering, and I wasn’t really keeping up with family. It got bad enough that they felt like they had to bring me in for help.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you're in a daze or spaced out when you're not having one of these blank moments?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I don’t think I usually feel spaced out unless one of those blank moments is happening. Most of the time I feel pretty aware of where I am.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you're not yourself or like things aren't real when you're not having one of those blank moments?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think so. I haven’t really had that feeling like I’m not myself or that things aren’t real, at least not that I’ve noticed. I’ll be looking at the forms or the paddle and I just can’t remember the steps I’ve done for years, and it takes me a while to feel clear again.\n\nDoctor: Are you experiencing these memory blanks or difficulties recalling familiar information in situations outside of your job as well?\n\nPatient: Mostly I notice it at work. That’s where it really scares me—like I’ll look at the road-closure forms or be holding the stop-slow paddle, and I suddenly can’t remember steps I’ve done for years. Outside of work, I’m not as sure, but the big blank moments are happening there.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel anxious or overwhelmed in situations outside of your job?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I mean, I do get stressed outside work sometimes, like anyone, but I haven’t noticed the same kind of overwhelming feeling the way I do at work. It’s harder for me to say.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel anxious when you're not at work?\n\nPatient: Not really that I can clearly say. I mean, I get tense sometimes when I’m not working, but I haven’t noticed that same anxious, overwhelmed feeling the way it happens at work.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel anxious when you're not working?\n\nPatient: I don’t really think so. I mean, I can get uneasy here and there, but I haven’t noticed it being a regular thing when I’m not working.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any other problems with your body, like numbness, weakness, or difficulty with coordination?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think so. I haven’t noticed numbness or weakness, and I’m not aware of any coordination problems.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you're outside of your body watching yourself when these blank moments happen?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think so. I wouldn’t describe it like I’m outside my body watching myself. It’s more just… blank, like I lose track for a bit.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel disconnected from yourself or your surroundings when these blank moments are not happening?\n\nPatient: Not that I’ve noticed. I mean, outside of those blank times, I usually feel like I’m just here and aware of what’s around me. I don’t really feel disconnected like that.\n\nDoctor: Have these memory blanks affected your relationships or daily activities outside of your job?\n\nPatient: Yes. It’s been a problem. I wasn’t reliable at work anymore because I couldn’t finish the road-closure paperwork, and I didn’t trust myself with the traffic-safety steps.\n\nOutside of work, I pulled back from things too. I stopped going to choir and volunteering, and I wasn’t really keeping up with family. It got bad enough that they felt like they had to bring me in for help.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you're in a daze or spaced out when you're not having one of these blank moments?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I don’t think I usually feel spaced out unless one of those blank moments is happening. Most of the time I feel pretty aware of where I am.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you're not yourself or like things aren't real when you're not having one of those blank moments?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think so. I haven’t really had that feeling like I’m not myself or that things aren’t real, at least not that I’ve noticed. Outside of the blanks, things feel normal in that way.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're disconnected from your thoughts or feelings when you're not at work?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d put it that way. When I’m not at work, I don’t really notice feeling cut off from my thoughts or feelings. I might be quiet or not very motivated, but not disconnected exactly.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n16\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF48.1, R41.0, G31.84\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: I came in because my mind keeps going blank at really bad times. Like, I’ll be looking at the road-closure forms, or even holding the stop-slow paddle, and suddenly I can’t remember the steps I’ve done for years. It’s been scaring me.\n\nDoctor: Do these blank moments seem to happen when you're under a lot of stress or pressure?\n\nPatient: Yes, it does feel worse when there’s pressure. Like if I’m looking at the road-closure forms or I’ve got the stop-slow paddle in my hand, my mind just goes blank, and I suddenly can’t remember steps I’ve done for years. That’s what scares me.\n\nDoctor: Do these blank moments and memory losses last for a while after the pressure or stress is gone?\n\nPatient: It can last a bit, yeah. Even after the pressure eases, I’m still kind of stuck, like my mind hasn’t caught up. I’ll be looking at the forms or the paddle and I just can’t remember the steps I’ve done for years, and it takes me a while to feel clear again.\n\nDoctor: Are you experiencing these memory blanks or difficulties recalling familiar information in situations outside of your job as well?\n\nPatient: Mostly I notice it at work. That’s where it really scares me—like I’ll look at the road-closure forms or be holding the stop-slow paddle, and I suddenly can’t remember steps I’ve done for years. Outside of work, I’m not as sure, but the big blank moments are happening there.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel anxious or overwhelmed in situations outside of your job?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I mean, I do get stressed outside work sometimes, like anyone, but I haven’t noticed the same kind of overwhelming feeling the way I do at work. It’s harder for me to say.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel anxious when you're not at work?\n\nPatient: Not really that I can clearly say. I mean, I get tense sometimes when I’m not working, but I haven’t noticed that same anxious, overwhelmed feeling the way it happens at work.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel anxious when you're not working?\n\nPatient: I don’t really think so. I mean, I can get uneasy here and there, but I haven’t noticed it being a regular thing when I’m not working.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any other problems with your body, like numbness, weakness, or difficulty with coordination?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think so. I haven’t noticed numbness or weakness, and I’m not aware of any coordination problems.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you're outside of your body watching yourself when these blank moments happen?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think so. I wouldn’t describe it like I’m outside my body watching myself. It’s more just… blank, like I lose track for a bit.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel disconnected from yourself or your surroundings when these blank moments are not happening?\n\nPatient: Not that I’ve noticed. I mean, outside of those blank times, I usually feel like I’m just here and aware of what’s around me. I don’t really feel disconnected like that.\n\nDoctor: Have these memory blanks affected your relationships or daily activities outside of your job?\n\nPatient: Yes. It’s been a problem. I wasn’t reliable at work anymore because I couldn’t finish the road-closure paperwork, and I didn’t trust myself with the traffic-safety steps.\n\nOutside of work, I pulled back from things too. I stopped going to choir and volunteering, and I wasn’t really keeping up with family. It got bad enough that they felt like they had to bring me in for help.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you're in a daze or spaced out when you're not having one of these blank moments?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I don’t think I usually feel spaced out unless one of those blank moments is happening. Most of the time I feel pretty aware of where I am.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you're not yourself or like things aren't real when you're not having one of those blank moments?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think so. I haven’t really had that feeling like I’m not myself or that things aren’t real, at least not that I’ve noticed. It’s mostly affected work, but it spilled over into everything. I stopped being reliable because I couldn’t finish the road-closure paperwork or trust myself with the traffic-safety steps anymore.\n\nAnd outside work, I kind of pulled back from people. Even after the pressure eases, I’m still kind of stuck, like my mind hasn’t caught up. That’s where it really scares me—like I’ll look at the road-closure forms or be holding the stop-slow paddle, and I suddenly can’t remember steps I’ve done for years. I mean, I get tense sometimes when I’m not working, but I haven’t noticed that same anxious, overwhelmed feeling the way it happens at work.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel anxious when you're not working?\n\nPatient: I don’t really think so. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there.   },

    "role": "user",

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I think it’s more like my mind just goes blank, even with things I know how to do.
